CABINET SECRETARIAT 
(Deportment of Statistics) 

New Delhi, the 6th February, 1973 
NOTIFICATION 

S O. 585. — In pursuance of sub-rule (2) of rule 9, clause 
(b) of sub-rule (2) of rule 12 and sub-rule (1) of rule 24, 
read with rule 34, of the Central Civil Services (Classification, 
Control and Appeal) Rules 1965, the President hereby makes 
the following further amendments In the Schedule to the 
notification of the Government of India in the Cabinet Sec- 
retariat No. S.R.O. 633, dated the 28th February 1957, 
namely:— 

In the Schedule to the said notification, in part I — 
General Central Service, Class II, under the head- 
ing "National Sample Survey Organisation 1 ’, against 
the entry “All Gazetted posts" in column 1, for 
the word “Secretary” in the corresponding entry 
in column 2, the words "Chief Executive Officer" 
shall be substituted. 

[No. C-l 1012/1/70-NSS. I] 
H, L, KOHLI, Under Secy.

8*0* 587. — In exercise of the powers conferred by sub- 
section (1) of Section 5, read with section 6, of the Delhi 
Special Police Establishment Act, 1946 (25 of 1946) the 
Central Government hereby makes the following amend- 
ment in the Orders of the Government of India in the 
Ministry of Home Affairs /Department of Personnel, 

No. 25/12/62-AVD IT, dated the 18th February, 1963. 

No. 25/12/62-AVD, dated the 16th January, 1965. 

No. 228/1/65-AVD II, dated the 12th August, 1965. 

No, 25/9/64- AVD, dated the 6th August, 1966. 

No. 25/4/64-AVD II, dated the 21st November, 1967. 

No, 228/3/66-AVD (II), dated the 10th July, 1970. 

No. 228 /7/65-AVD (II), dated the 13th July, 1970* 

No. 228/4/66-AVD (II), dated the 13th July, 1970. 

No. 228/6/67-AVD (n), dated the 15th July, 1970. 

No. 228 /1 1/67- AVD U, dated the 3rd September, 1970. 

namely:— \ 

In the said Orders, for the word 'Assam’ wherever it 
occurs, the words ‘Assam/Meghalaya’ shall be substituted. 

[No. 228/4/72- AVD-II (II)]

ELECTION COMMISSION OF INDIA 

New Delhi, the 29th January, 1973 

ORDER 

S.O* 588* — Whereas the Election Commission is satis- 
fied that Shri Dula Ram S/o Shri Kbeta Ram, Post Chirawa, 
District Jhunjhnnu, Rajasthan, a contesting candidate for 
election to the House of the People from 3-Jhunjhunu Par- 
liamentary Constituency, has failed to lodge an account of 
his election expenses within the time and in the manner 
as required by the Representation of the People Act, 1951, 
and the Rules made thereunder; 


Sec. 3(ii)] 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MARCH 3,1973/PHALGUNA 12, 1894 


951 


2, And whereas, the said candidate even after the notices 
has not given any reason or explanation for the failure and 
the Election Commission is satisfied that he has no good 
reason or justification for such failure ; 

3. Now, therefore, in pursuance of section 10 A of the 
said Act, the Election Commission hereby declares the said 
Shri Dula Ram to be disqualified for being chosen as 
and for being a member of either House of Parliament or of 
the Legislative Assembly or Legislative Council of a State 
for a period of three years from the date of this order. 

[No. RJ-HP/3/71]

ORDER 

S.O. 589. — Whereas the Election Commission is satis- 
fied that Shrimati Taravati W/o Shri Tormal, Village Ram- 
pura, Post Rampura-Beri, District Churu, Rajasthan, a com 
testing candidate for election to the House of the People from 
3-Jhunjhunu Parliamentary Constituency, has failed to lodge 
an account of her election expen sOs as required by the Repre- 
sentation of the People Act, 1951, and the Rules made 
thereunder ; 

2. And whereas, the said candidate even after the notices 
has not given any reason or explanation for the failure and 
the Election Commission is satisfied that he has no good 
reason or justification for such failure ; 

3. Now, therefore, in pursuance of section 10 A of the 
said Act, the Election Commission hereby declares the said 
Shrimati Taravati to be disqualified for being chosen as 
and for being a member of either House of Parliament or of 
the Legislative Assembly or Legislative Council of a State 
for a period of three years from the date of this order.

ORDER 

.:v 

S.O, 590. — Wliereas the Election Commission is satis- 
fied that Shri Patel Manorbhai Viramdas, At and Post 
Jakhana, Taluka Chanasma, District Mehsana (Gujarat), a 
contesting candidate for election held in March, 1972, to 
the Gujarat Legislative Assembly from 82-Chana^ma consti- 
tuency, has failed to lodge an account of hia election 
expenses, as required by the Representation of the People 
Act, 1951, and the Rules made thereunder; 

AND WHEREAS the said candidate, even after due 
notices, has not given any reason or explanation for the 
failure and the Election Commission is satisfied that he has 
no good reason or justification for such failure; 

NOW, THEREFORE, in pursuance of section 10A of the 
said Act, the Election Commission hereby declares the said 
Shri Patel Manorbhai Viramdas to be disqualified for being 
chosen as, and for being, a member of either House of 
Parliament or of the Legislative Assembly or Legislative 
Council of a State for period of three years from the date 
of this Order.

New Delhi, the 1st February, 1973 
ORDER 

8 ,0. 591. — Whereas the Election Commission is satis- 
fled that Shri S. Muralidhar Rao, House No. 4-4-107, Pedda- 
palli, Karimnagar District (Andhra Pradesh), a contesting 
candidate for general election to the Andhra Pradesh Legis- 
lative Assembly held in March, 1972 from 244-Peddapalli 
constituency, has failed to lodge an account of his election 
expenses at all as required by the Representation of the 
People Act, 1951, and the Rules made thereunder; 

AND WHEREAS the said candidate, even after due 
notices, has not given any reason or explanation for the 
failure and the Election Commission is further satisfied that 
he has no good reason or justification for such failure; 

NOW, THEREFORE, in pursuance of section 10A of the 
said Act, the Election Commission hereby declares the said 
Shri S. Muralidhar Rao to be disqualified for being 
chosen as, and for being, a member of either House of 
Parliament or of Legislative Assembly or Legislative Council 
of a State for a period of three years from date of this 
Order.

New Delhi, the 5th February, 1973 
ORDER 

S.O. 592. — Whereas the Election Commission is satisfied 
that Shri Patel Muljibhai Nanalal, Mandap Contractor, 
Kapad Bazar, Bhavnagar (Gujarat), a contesting candidate 
for the general election held in March, 1972, to the Gujarat 
Legislative Assembly from 51-Gogho Constituency, has failed 
to lodge his account of election expenses in the manner 
required by the Representation of the People Act, 1951, and 
the Rules made thereunder; 

AND WHEREAS, the said candidate, even after due notices, 
has not given any reason or explanation for the failure and 
the Election Commission is satisfied that he has no good 
reason or justification for such failure; 


NOW THEREFORE, in pursuance of section 10A of the 
said Act, the Election Commission hereby declares the said 
Shri Patel Muljibhai Nanalal to be disqualified for being 
chosen as, and for being, a member of cither House of 
Parliament or of the Legislative Assembly or Legislative 
Council of a state for a period of three years from the date 
of this Order.

Taluk a Akalkot, District Sholapur (Maharashtra), a contesting 
candidate for the general election held in March, 1972, to 
the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly from 200-Ak.alkot 
constituency, has failed to lodge an account of his election 
expenses in the manner required by the Representation of 
the People Act, 1951, and the Rules made thereunder; 

2. And whereas, the said candidate even after due notices 
has not given any reason or explanation for the failure and 
the Election Commission is further satisfied that he has no 
good reason or justification for such failure ; 

_3. Now, therefore, in pursuance of section 10 A of the 
said Act, the Election Commission hereby declares the said 
Shri Gokhale Keshav Damodar to be disqualified for being 
chosen as, and for being, a member of either House of Parlia- 
ment or of the Legislative Assembly or Legislative Council of 
State for a period of three years from the date of this order. 

[No. MT-LA/200/72 (2)] 
A. N. SEN, Secy.

COLLECTORATE AND DEPUTY COLLECTOR OF CUSTOM AND CENTRAL EXCISE 

Jaipur, the 12th February, 1973 
Central Excise 

S.O. 621. — In exercise of the powers conferred upon me under rules 15 and 16 of the Central Excise rules, 1944 and in partial 
modification of Notification issued under this Office C. No. V(4)30/l/Int./68/in/23752-23783 dated the 6th December, 1968 and C. No. 
V ( 4 ) 33/1 /Int/68/III/50— 86 dated 2-1-69, , I hereby notify that no declaration will be necessary under the said rules in respect of unmanu- 
factured tobacco grown in the areas not exceeding the limits specified in column 7 of the sub-joined schedule or cured in quantities not 
exceeding the limits indicated in column 8 of the said schedule in whole of the revenue jurisdiction of the respective revenue tchsils men 
tloned in column 5 of the Central Excise Division Jodhpur.

MINISTRY OF COMMERCE 
ORDER 

New Delhi, the 20th February, 1973 

S-O. 624.— In exercise of the powers conferred by sub-rule (2) of rule, 9 clause (b) of sub-rule(2) of rulo 12and sub-rule (1) 
of rule 24 of the Central Civil Services (Classification .Control and Appeal) Rules, 1965, and in supersession of the late Ministry of Com- 
merce and Consumer Industries’ Notification No. S.R.O. 631, dated the 28th February, 1957, the President hereby directs that— 

(1) in respect of the posts in the General Central Sendee, Class II, specified in column (1) of Part I of the Schedule to this Order 
the authority specified in column (2) shall be the Appointing Authority and the authority specified in column (3) shall be the Disciplinary 
Authority in regard to the penalties specified in colutnn (4) ; y ^ 

(2) in respect of the posts in the General Central Service, Class III and the General Central Service, Class IV, specified in column 
(1) of Part II and Part III of the said Schedule respectively, the authority specified in column (2) shall be the Appointing Authority and 
the authorities specified in columns (3) and (!) shall be the Disciplinary Authority and Appellate Authority respectively in regard to the 
penalties specified in column (4) .

[4. 6 (5) /71-fa\ fa. 7VT fa. 4.] 
New Delhi, the 3rd March, 1973 

8.0. 625. — In exercise of the powers conferred by section 
17 of the Export (Quality Control and Inspection) Act, 1963 
(22 of 1963), the Central Government hereby makes the 
following rules to amend the Export of Coir Products 
(Inspection) Rules, 1965, namely : — 

1. (i) These rules may be Called theExport of Coir Pro- 
ducts (Inspection) Amendment Rules, 1973. 

(ii) They shall come into force on the date of their pub- 
lication in the official gazette. 

2. In the Export of Coir Products (Inspection) Rules, 1965, 
for sub-rule (4) of rule 4, the following rule shall be sub- 
stituted namely ; — 

“(4) After satisfying itself that the consignment of Coir 
Products conforms to the recognised specifications the agency 


shall within three days of the receipt of the intimation and 
the particulars of the consignment under sub-rule (1) of rule 
4, issue a certificate to the exporter declaring the consignment 
as conforming to the recognised specifications and is export- 
worthy. 

Provided that where the agency is not so satisfied, it shall 
within the said period of three days refuse to issue such 
certificate and communicate such refusal to the exporter along- 
with the reasons therefor. 1 * 

[No, 6(5)/7 l-ET Sc EP]

MINISTRY OF COMMERCE 
NOTIFICATION 


(Office of the Chief Controller of Imports and Exports) 
ORDER 


New Delhi, the 3rd March, 1973 

S.O. 626. — In exercise of the powers conferred by section 
17 of the Export (Quality Control and Inspection) Act, 1963 
(22 of 1963), the Central Government hereby makes the 
following rules to amend the Export of Coir Yarn 
(Inspection) Rules, 1966, namely: — 

1. (i) These rules may be called the Export of Coir Yarn 
(Inspection) Amendment Rules, 1973. 

(ii) They shall come into force on the date of their pub- 
lication in the official gazette. 

2. In the Export of Coir Yam (Inspection) Rules, 1966, 
for rule 7, the following rule shall be substituted, namely ; — 

M 7. Certificate of Inspection — After satisfying itself that the 
consignment of Coir Yarn conforms to the recognised speci- 
fications and has been packed and labelled in accordance with 
the instructions issued in this behalf, the agency shall within 
three days of the receipt of the intimation and the parti- 
culars of the ^consignment under sub-rule (l) of rule 4. issue 
a certificate to the exporter declaring that the consignment 
conforms to the recognised specification and is export- 
worthy. 

Provided that Where the agency is not so satisfied, it shall 
within the said period of three days refuse to issue such 
certificate and communicate such refusal to the exporter 
alongwith the reasons therefore/* 

[No. 6(5)/71-EI & EP] 

M. K. B. BHATNAGAR, Dy. Director, 
(Export Promotion).

New Delhi, the 17th February, 1973 

S.O, 627. — M/s. Piem Hotels Pvt. Ltd., Neel Kanth, 
98, Marine Drives, Bombay-2 were granted an import licence 
No. P/A/1357417 dated 13-10-71 for Rs. 10,000 (Rupees 
Ten Thousand only). They have applied for the issue of 
duplicate Exchange Control Purposes copy of the said licence 
on the ground that the original Exchange Control copy has 
been lost. It is further stated that the original Exchange 
Control Copy was registered with the Customs authorities 
at Bombay and utilised partly. It was utilised for 
Rs. 9,672.68 and the balance available on it was Rs. 327.32. 

2. In support of this contention, the applicant has filed an 
affidavit on a stamped paper, I am accordingly satisfied that 
the original Exchange Control Purposes copy of the said 
licence has been lost. Therefore in exercise of the powers 
conferred under Sub-clause 9(ccJ of the imports (Control) 
Order 1955 dated 7-12-1955 as amended, the said original 
Exchange Control Purposes copy of Licence No. P/A/ 
1357417 dated 13-10-71 issued to M/s. Piem Hotels Pvt. 
Ltd., Bombay is hereby cancelled. 

3. A duplicate Exchange Control Purposes Copy of the 
said licence is being issued separately to the licensee. 

[No. 469-H/AM-72/ILS/3943] 
M, G. GOMBAR, Dy. Chief Controller.

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 21st November, 1972 

S.O- 633. — M/s. Wings Wear Pvt. Ltd., 1/4, West 
Patel Nagar, New Delhi were granted Import licence No. P/ 
L/2620162/C, dated 30-3-1971 for Rs. 73,995 for import 
of Coal tar Dyes (Permissible items) as per App, 24 of Vol. 

I of AM, 71, Red Book and other items. They have filed 
an affidavit as required under Para. 38 read with App. 8 
of Import Trade Control Hand Book of Rules & Procedure, 
1972-73 where in they have stated that Exchange Control 
Purpose copy of Licence No. P/L /2620162/C for 

Rs. 73,995 has been lost /misplaced having been registered 
with Bombay Custom House and utilised partly. The total 
amount for which licence was issued is Rs. 73,995 and total 
amount for which the original copy was utilised is Rs. 55,460. 
The Duplicate copy now required is to cover the balance of 
Rs. 18,535, 

2. I am satisfied that Exchange Control copy of the said 
licence has been lost/misplaced. 

3. In exercise of the powers conferred on me under 
subject Clause 9(C) in the Import Trade Control Order 1955 
dated 7-12-1955 as amended upto date, the Exchange Con- 
trol copy of the said licence No, P/L/2620162/C, dated 
30-3-1971 for Rs. 73,995 is hereby cancelled. 

4. The applicant is now being issued a duplicate Ex- 
change control copy of the licence in accordance with the 
provision of para, 318(4) of Import Trade Control Hand 
Book of Rules & Procedure, 1972-73 for remittance purposes 
only.

New Delhi, the 15th February 1973. 


SO. 638. — Whereas it appears to the Central Gover- 
nment that It is necessary in the public interest that for the trans- 
port of Petroleum Products between the Haldia Port (District- 
Midnapore) and Rajbandh Delivery Point (District-Burdwan) 
of Indian Oil Corporation Limited (Pipelines) in West Bengal 
States, pipelines should be laid by the Indian Oil Corporation 
Limited (Pipelines) and that for the purpose of laying such pipe 
lines it is necessary to acquire the right of user in the land descri 
bed in the Schedule annexed hereto ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub- 
section (1) of Section 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines (Acquisition 
of Right User in land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962) the Central Go- 
vernment hereby declares its intention to acquire the right of 
user therein. 

Any person interested in the said land may within 21 (Twenty- 
one) days from the date of this Notification object to the laying 
of pipelines under the land, to Competent Authority at 14, Lee 
Road, Calcutta-20, in the office of the Indian Oil Corporation 
Limited (Pipelines). Every person making such an objection 
shill ah:> state specifically whether he wishes to bo heard in 
person or by legal practitioner.

New Delhi, the 21st February, 1973 

S.O. 639. — WHEREAS by a notification of the Government 
of India in the Ministry of Petroleum and Chemicals (Depart- 
ment of Petroleum) S.O. No. 3179 dated 14-9-1972, under 
sub-section (1) of section 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines 
(Acquisition of Right of User in land) Act, 1962 (50 of 
1962), the Central Government declared its intention to 
acquire the Right of User in the lands specified in the 
schedule appended to that notification for the purpose of lay- 
ing pipelines; 

AND WHEREAS the Competent Authority has under sub- 
section (1) of section 6 of the said Act, submitted report to 
the Government; 


AND FURTHER WHEREAS the Central Government 
has after considering the said report, decided to acquire 
the right of user in the lands specified in the schedule ap- 
pended to this notification; 


NOW, THEREFORE, in exercise of the power conferre 
by sub-section (1) of the section 6 of the said Act, the Cei 
tral Government hereby declares that the right of user i 
the said lands specified in the schedule appended to th 
notification hereby acquired for laying the pipelines; 

52 G of 1/72 — 8 


AND FURTHER in exercise of the power conferred by 
sub-section (4) of that section, the Central Government 
directs (hat the right of user in the said lands shall instead 
of vesting in the Central Government vest on this date of 
the publication of this declaration in the Oil & Natural 
Gas Commission free from all encumbrances.

S.O. 640* — WHEREAS it appears to the Central Govern- 
ment that it is necessary in the public interest that for 
ound beding, wire laying and anode bed from Bunk to 
node bed and pipleline Cathodic Protection to KNK/ 
CALICO / K ALOL LINES be provided by the Oil and 
Natural Gas Commission : 

AND WHEREAS it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such lines for Cathodic Protection it is necessary to acquire 
the Right of User in the Land described in the schedule 
annexed hereto: 

NOW, THEREFORE, in exercise of the powers conferred 
by sub-section (i) of the Petroleum Pipelines (Acquisition 
of Right of User in land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the 

Central Government hereby declares the intention to acquire 
the right of user therein: 

PROVIDED THAT any person interested in the said land 
may, within 21 days from the date of this notification* 
object to the laying of the wire lines under the land to 
Competent Authority, Oil & Natural Gas Commission, Cons- 
truction & Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road, Baroda- 
9: 

AND EVERY person making such an objection shall also 
state specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person 
or by a legal practitioner.

S.O. 643. — WHEREAS by a notification of the Govt, of 
India in the Ministry of Petroleum and Chemicals (Depart- 
ment of Petroleum) S.O. No. 3751 dated 12-10-1972 under 
sub-section (1) of section 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines (Ac- 
quisition of Right of User in land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), 
the Central Government declared its intention to acquire the 
Right of User in the lands specified in the schedule appended 
to that notification for the purpose of laying pipelines; 

AND WHEREAS the Competent Authority has under 
sub-section (1) of section 6 of the said Act, submitted report 
to the Government; 

AND FURTHER WHEREAS the Central Government has 
after considering the said report, decided to acquire the right 
of user in the lands specified in the schedule appended to Inis 
notification; 

NOW, THEREFORE, in exercise of the power conferred 
by sub-section (1) of the section 6 of the said Act, the Cen- 
tral Government hereby declares that the right of user in 
the said lands specified in the schedule appended to this 
notification hereby acquired fpr .laying the pipelines; 

AND FURTHER in exercise of the power conferred by 
sub-section (4) of that section, the Central Government directs 
that the right of user in the said lands shall instead of 
vesting in the Central Government vest on this date of the 
publication of this declaration in the Oil & Natural Gas 
Commission free from all encumbrances.

S.O. 644. — WHEREAS by a notification of the Govt, of 
India in the Ministry of Petroleum and Chemicals (Depart- 
ment of Petroleum) S.O. No. 3181 dated 18-9-1972 under 
sub-section (1) of section 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines 
(Acquisition of Right of User in land) Act, 1962 (50 of 
1962), the Central Government declared jts intention to 
acquire the Right of User in the lands specified in the 
schedule appended to that notification for the purpose 
of laying pipelines; 

AND WHEREAS the Competent Authority has under 
sub-section (1) of section 6 of the said Act, submitted report 
to the Government; 



1009 


Sec- 3(ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MARCH 3, 1973/PHALGUNA 12, 1894 


AND FURTHER WHEREAS the Central Government has 
after considering the said report, decided to acquire the right 
of user in the lands specified in the schedule appended to 
this notification; 

NOW, THEREFORE, in exercise of the power conferred 
by sub-section (1) of the section 6 of the said Act, the 
Central Government hereby declares that the right of user in 
the said lands specified in the schedule appended to this 
notification hereby acquired for laying the pipelines; 

AND FURTHER in exercise of the power conferred by 
subjection (4) of that section, the Central Government directs 
that the right of user in the said lands shall instead of 
vesting in the Central Government vest on this date of the 
publication of this declaration in the Oil & Natural Gas 
Commission free from all encumbrances.

lations, 1955, as amended from time to lime, the Indian Standards Institution hereby notifies that licences No. CM/L-352, CM/1,-413,

have been granted to the new firm operative from 1 Jan 1973 : —

S.O. 660. — Whereas Ihe Central Government have in pur- 
suance of the provisions of clause (a) of sub-section (1) 
of section 3 of the Indian Medical Council Act, 1956 (102

S.O. 668. — In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby publishes the following award of the Central Gov- 
ernment Industrial Tribunal, Jabalpur, in the Industrial dis- 
pute between the employers in relation to the management 
of Kotma Colliery of Messrs. Associated Cement Company 
Limited, Post Office Kotma Colliery, District Shahdol 
(Madhya Pradesh), and their workmen which was received 
by the Central Government on the 13th February, 1973. 

[No.L| 22 1 1 1 1 17 1 *LRII] 

KARNAIL SINGH, Under Secy. 

CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL 
CUM LABOUR COURT, JABALPUR 

Dated January, 29, 1973 

Present : 

MR. JUSTICE S. N. KATJU PRESIDING OFFI- 

CER 

Case Ref. No. CGn7LC(RX29)/72 
(Notification No. L|2211[l|71-LR.n, dated 28-7-1972) 

Parties : 

Employers in relation to the management of Kotma 
Colliery of Messrs. Associated Cement Company 
Limited and their workmen represented through the 
Kotma Colliery Mazdoor Sabba, P.O, Kotma Colliery, 
Dlstrict-Shabdol (M.P.). 

52 G of 1/72—14 


Appearances i 

For employers S/Sri P. S. Nair, Advocate and 

V. S. Mathur, Administrative Officer , 

For workmen None 

Industry : Coal Mine District : Shahdol (M. P.) 

AWARD 


This is a reference under Section 10 of the I.D. Act 1947, 
The question referred to is:— 

Whether the management of Kotma Colliery of Messrs. 
Associated Cement Company Limited are justified 
in withholding the annual increments duo on the 
15th August, 1970 and on the 15th August, 1971 
of the workers mentioned in Annexure A and B7 
If, not, to what relief are the workers entitled to ? 

The names of 81 workmen have been mentioned In Anne- 
xtire 'A* and 29 workmen have been mentioned in Anne- 
xure *B' to the Schedule of the reference. 

No written statement has been filed on behalf of the 
workmen, nor anyone has appeared before the Trbunal on 
their behalf. The management has filed its written state- 
ment. The maintainability of the reference has been chal- 
lenged, It is further stated on behalf of the management 
that the action of the management in with- 
holding the annual increments of the workmen con- 
cerned was absolutely fair and proper and was fully justified. 
The management has contented that during the years end- 
ing 15th August, 1970 and 15th August, 1971 the attendance 
of the workmen concerned was very poor and the manage- 
ment was, therefore, justified in withholding their annual 
increments. Shri V. S. Mathur has given evidence on behalf 
of the management. He has produced copies of the notices 
dated July 29/30, 1970 and December 28(30, 1970 (Ex.E(l 
and E|2) which contain the Rules with regard to the num- 
ber of attendance days for puiposes of earning the annual 
increments. The notice dated 28] 3 0-1 2- 1970 says that the:— 

1. “The object of our fixing 240 days attendance as 
qualifying attendance for purposes of earning an- 
nual increments was to reduce and if possible to 
eliminate the high degree of absenteeism that is 
now prevalent. Such absenteeism imposes a great 
strain on the management 

3. Though the qualifying attendance has been fixed 

at 240 days, due consideration will be made for 
the following factors if they have contributed 
to employees* inability to put in the qualifying 
attendance: 

(a) Leave with wages. 

(b) Employees sustaining disability, due to an acci- 
dent within the meaning of the workmen's com- 
pensation Act. 

(c) Employee's availing of maternity leave. 

(d) Sick leave with wages. 

4. The special causes shown: circumstances explained by 

the employee in response to show cause issued to 
him will be carefully considered before a decision 
is taken to stop his increment." 

The notice dated July 29/30, 1970 said that: 

"1. Before imposing the aforesaid penalty, the Com- 
pany shall issue a show cause notice to the con- 
cerned workman informing him of the charges 



1054 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MARCH 3 


In respect of which it is proposed to impose the 
penalty. 

2. Workman concerned shall be given a reasonable 

opportunity of showing cause as to why the pro- 
posed penally should not be imposed. 

3. The workman concerned will have the right of ap- 

peal to the General Manager at the Company’s Head 
Office in Bombay again any order imposing the 
above penalty.” 

Shri V, S. Mathur has given eidcncc in support of the 
management’s cage. He has stated that the attendance of 

the workmen concerned fell short from the prescribed limit 
of 240 days during the period in question and consequently 
their annual increments were withheld. He further stated 

that it was within the competence of the management to 
terminate the services of the workmen conerned on the 
ground of their irregular attendance, but the drastic step of 
terminating their services was not taken and instead only 
their annual increments were withheld. He further slated 

that show cause notices were duly served on the workmen 
concerned to explain the reas'ons fer their absence from work 
but their explanations were found unsatisfactory. He also 
stated that none of the workmen exercised their right of 
appeal to the General Manager against the order with-hold- 
ing their increments. As mentioned above no one has 

appeared on behalf of the workmen and the aforesaid con- 
tentions of the management stand un-challenged. Since the 
workmen concerned were irregular in their attendance and 
under the Rules they were not entitled to annual increments 
the management was justified in with-holding the annual 
increments of the workmen cone cned which were due ou 
15th August, 1970 and 15th August, 1971. f, therefore, hold 
that the workmen mentioned in Anncxures ‘A’ and C B’ of 
the Schedule to the reference arc not entitled to any annual 
increments due on 15th August 1970 and 15lh August 1971 
and thus are not entitled to any relief. 

I have proceeded on the assumption that, the reference 
to this Tribunal is maintainable. I make my award ac- 
cordingly. I make no order as to costs. 

S. N. KATJU, P reading Officer 


New Delhi, 19 February, 1973 

S*0. 669. — In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby publishes the following award of the Central Gov- 
ernment Industrial Tribunal, Hyderabad, in the industrial 
dispute between the employers in relation to the management 
of Balempalti Division of Singarcni Collieries Company 
Limited, Post Office Belampalli (Andhra Pradesh), and their 
workmen, which was received by the Central Government 
on the 12th February, 1973, 

[No. 7/J8/70-LRTI] 
KARNAIL SINGH , Under Sccy. 

BEFORE THE INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL (CENTRAL) 
AT HYDERABAD 

Preient : 

Sri P. S. Ananth, B.Sc.,B.L., Industrial Tribunal, Hyderabad. 

Industrial BEputa No. 21 of 1972. 

BETWEEN 

Workmen of Singarcni Collieries Company Limited, Bell a m- 
palli and Ramafcrishnapur Divisions. 

AND 

Management of Singarcni Collieries Company Limited, 
Bellampalli and Rumakrishnapur Divisions. 

Appearances : 

Sri S. Nagaiah Reddy, President, Tandur Coal Mines 
Labour Union, Bellampalli for Workmen. 

Sri M, Shyam Mohan, Personnel Officer, S, C. Co. Ltd., 
Bellampalli, for Management. 


1973/PHALGUNA 12, 1894 [Part II— 


AWARD 


The Government of India, Ministry of Labour, Employment 
and Rehabilitation (Department of Labour and Employment) 
by its Order No, 7/18/70-LR.U dated 5-1-1971 referred the 
following dispute under Section 10(1) (d) of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (hereinafter referred to as the said Act) 
to this Tribunal for adjudication namely: 

“Whether the management of Bellampalli and Rama- 
krishnapur Divisions of Messrs. Singarcni Collieries 
Company Limited, is justified in placing Sarvashri 
Nagula Chandigarh, Kannaboyina Posham, Kusina 
Lingaiah, Dandi Mondi, Gujja Bucham, Band 
Saw Cutters in Category III under the new Wage 
Board Recommendations? If not, to what relief 
are they entitled and from what date?” 

This reference was taken on file an Industrial Dispute 
No. 21 of 1971 and notices were issued to tho parties. 
For the purpose of convenience the Workmen of Singarcni 
Collieries Company Limited, Bellampalli and Ramakrishnapur 
Divisions are referred to as the petitioners and the Singarcni 
Collieries Company limited, Bellampalli and Ramakrishnapur 
Divisions are referred lo as the respondent in the course of 
this award. 


2, The claimants are represented by the Tandur Coal 
Mines Labour Union, Bellampalli (hereinafter referred to 
as the said Union) and the President of the said Union 
filed the claims statement contending as follows: — The 
Management have implemented the recommendations of the 
Central Wage Board for Coal Minim; Industry with effect 
from 15-8-1967. The claimants were fitted into Category 
No, III after the Wage Beard. These workmen were in old 
Category V and they were working as Band Saw Cutters. 
Their duties include cutting timber Jogs, soldering the saw, 
sharpening the teeth of the saw and adjusting. Thus their 
work include that of carpentry and black rmithy. The Wage 
Hoard did not specifically recommend any category to the 
Band Saw Cutters a3 such. They must be given the grade 
or category according to the nature of work. Prior to Wage 
Board these workmen were drawing more than the Carpen- 
ters or Blacksmiths. The Management by fitting them into 
the new Category Iff treated them as mazdoors or atleast 
unskilled workers. The Management fitted the workmen who 
arc performing the jobs of carpentry, blacksmithy etc. in 
Category IV (new). So the claimants are also entitled to 
be fitted in Category IV (new) with effect from 15-8-1967. 

3, The respondent filed its counter statement contending as 
follows: The Management is at liberty to implement the 
recommendations of the Central Wage Board for Coal Mining 
Industi y with v view to see that the various categories of 
workmen arc suitably placed under a proper category but 
at the same time the recommendations are not statutory. The 
management has rightly placed the concerned workmen in cate- 
gory ITT with their designations as sawyers. That the compari- 
son of Sawyers, to that of Carpenters, Blacksmithy and sharp- 
eners is invidious and the claim of the workmen cannot be 
exaggerated. When it is admitted by the workmen them- 
selves that in file Wage Board recommendations no specific 
grade is recon mended for Sawyers, the management is 
the appropriate authority to fit them in appropriate category 
and on the principles of equity, fair play and justice, the 
management, pit 1 ;ed these workmen, who were getting Category 
V(old) into Ccfiegory Ill(new). It has no effect that the said 
workmen are yerforming the jobs of skilled nature and 
therefore to m vke invidious distinctions is to side-track the 
issues involved. The essential nature of the duties involved 
is cutting of timber and sharpening of saw blades by filing. 
Hence the claim is liable to be dismissed. 

4. The dispute that is referred for adjudication is whether 
the Management of Bellampalli and Ramakrishnapur Divi- 
sion of Singarcni Collieries Company Limited is justified in 
placing Nagula Chandraiah, Kannaboyina Posham, Kusina 
Lingaiah, Dandi Mondi and Gujja Bucham, who are Band 
Saw Cutters in Category III (new) under the Wajie Board 
recommendations? 

5. Die case of the petitioner is that the claimants, who 
were in category V(old) and who were working as Band 
haw Cutters prior to the recommendations of the Central 



1055 


Sec. 3(ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MARCH 3,1973/PHA LGUNA 12, 1894 


Wage Board for the Coal Mining Industry (hereinafter re- 
ferred to as the Wage Board) were doing the duties of 
cutting timber Jogs, soldering the tiaw, sharpening the teeth 
of the saw blades and that as their work induced that of 
carpentry and biacksmithy and sharpening they should have 
been fitted in Category IV (new) whereas the Management 
fitted them in category III (new) treating them as mazdoors 
or atlcast as unskilled workers. The contentions or tke res- 
pondent are that there is no question o£ compassion of the 
work of the claimants with that of Carpenters, Blacksmiths 
and Sharpeners, that considering the nature of the work per- 
formed by the claimants they were rightly placed in Category 
III (new) giving them the designation as Sawyers mid that 
their essential work is only that cf cutting of timber and that 
the Wage Board has not recommended any specific grade for 
Sawyers. It is common ground that there is no mention of 
any Band-Saw Cutters or Sawyers in the 1 ©commendations of 
the Wag© Board. 

6, W. W. 1 (Mavnla Chandraiah) is one of the claim- 
ants. He says that prior to Ids joining this Company there 
was no Band-Saw Cutters that they cut the timber according 
to size they require, that they have to sharpen iho teeth of 
the saw with the help of the grinding stone, that those who 
were working as Bit Sharpeners on the grinding stones were 
in old Cateogry III and after Wage Board they wero placed 
in new Category IV and if saw blade breaks they have to 
sharpen the broken edges and take them tc the Blaksmith’s 
place and heat them in the furnace and join them, that 
when there were no grinding stones at the Blacksmiths place 
previously the sharpening of saw teeth and joining the broken 
edges ol the saw were done at the workshop itself by the 
persons there, that the Blacksmiths were previously in Old 
Category VI and old Category VII and now those who were 
in old Category VI are new Category V and those who wero 
in old Category VII are now in new Category VI, that as there 
wn9 delay in the work when the saw blades were being 
sent to the workshop, they were asked to learn the work 
of sharpening the teeth of the saw blades and of joining 
the broken saw blades so that they can attend to this work 
immediately and so they have been doing this work, that 
they requested the Company to give them higher category 
considering the nalnro of the work done by them, that 
Iho Blacksmiths and those who were in old category III 
were given new Category IV, that the Wage Board decs 
not say any thing about the Band-Saw Cutter, that there is 
lot of risk in the work that they do, that the machines would 
be running when they cut the timber with saw and the 
danger of the saw cutting the palm and the lingers is there 
and so they are asking for new Category IV. In his cross 
examination^ he says (hat when he joined service he was 
given old Category V that prior to that he was working 
under the Contractor, that when the Company took him 
and others into service and they gave old Category V and 
they first did only the timber ending work, that while doing 
the timber cutting work they learnt* the work of sharpening 
the saw teeth and of joining the broken saw blades in the 
timber yard, that if there is no timber cuffing work com- 
pletely then only they go and lift the timber and place them 
on tlie shoulders of persons who carry the timber logs, that 
after the Wage Board the Company gave raw Category Til 
for those who were in old Categories IV and V, ‘that ho 
does not know whether after ihc Wage Board the Company 
issued any circular designating them as Sawyers, that after 
the Wage Board they ure being called only as Cutfore and 
the pay sheet also shows this, (hat they arc asking for new 
Category IV though there is no mention of Band Saw 
Cutters in the Wage Board, considering the nature of their 
work, that he does not know about Kothauudcm and Yeltandu 
and that the persons doing Ihc s:mc work like him me only 
in new Category III, that he does not know whether tha 
Govci nment turned down any request of any Sawyers in 
Ycllandn and Kothn^udcm, when they asked for new Cr.ic- 
gory IV, that there is no comparison of his work with the 
work of Blacksmith, that ho has nothing to do with the 
Ca.rpcnlcry work, that when he was in old Catcgorv V the 
Carpenters and Blacksmiths were in the eaf;-\mGof' VJ and 
VII, that the claimants Kurina Lingiah, Dendi Mond/, Gtijin. 
p Lien am are work mg in Kamakriabnapur and they are f.’ijo 
m new Category HI and that there is more of timber cutting 
work as cutter. 


J' Thomas) is working as Divisional 1 

sonnel Officer, Mandamari and Ramakrishnapur Cfivisk 
He save that out of the claimants D H Mondi, K usina L hr: 
and Cj. Bucham are working in Ramakrisbnapiir hi 


Building Department in the Timber Yard as Sawyers, that 
previously they wore known as Band-Saw Cutters, that after the 
implementation of the Wage Board recommendations they wero 
given Category TU(new), that Ex. Ml is the copy of the 
wage fixation from relating to K. JLingaiah and D. Mondi, that 
their nature of job is not like Carpenters or Blacksmiths, 
that there is no designation as Band Saw Cutters in the Wage 
Board Recommendations, that old Category V is equivalent 
to new Category HI and so the said claimants were fixed in 
new Category )U, that other persons in other sections who 
were in old category V were fixed similarly in new Cate- 
gory ITT, that similar demand of the present one came up 
from Kothagudein Division and the Government sent the 
letter EX.M2 and it shows that their demand was turned 
down, tli at the work of the claimants has no sort of con- 
nection with the work done by the Timbermen under ground, 
that new Category IV is given only to those who were in 
old Category VI and VII that the nature of the work done 
by the claimants has nothing to do with the skilled operations 
and so they are not entitled to new Category IV. In his cross 
examination it is elicited that while implementing the Wage 
Board all the Tradesmen in old Categories IV and V were 
given new Category IV and those who were in old Cate- 
gory VI and VII were given new Category V and 
those who were in old Categories VIII and IX 
were given new Category Vl that nothing is mentioned about 
Band Saw Cutters in the Coal Award (Coal Award referred to 
Mnzumdar Award) or in the Wage Board, that he does not 
know on what basis these Sawyers were fixed in okl cate- 
gory V and he docs not know whether the Sawyers gave any 
applications to the Management in December, 1968 after the 
issue of the circular by the Management claiming new Cate- 
gory IV. 

8. M.W.2 (V, K. Naragundkar) is working in Bcllampalli 
Divisions as Store Keeper and he also worked as Store 
Keeper in Kothagudcm. He says that in the Sub-Stores 
at Bcllampalli there are two Sawyers, namely, K Chandraiah 
and K. Poaham, who are also claimants in this case, that 
their pro Wage Board Category was Category V, that they are 
working since September, 1964, that after implementation of 
Wage Board they were placed in Category III (new) that 
the Company has given them designation ns Sawyers, that 
this designation is not covered by the Wage Board recommen- 
dations, "that the work of these Sawyers is to cut the big 
logs with Hand -Saw and they cut the smaller logs with 
circular saw, fhrt there rae three Sawyers in Rnmnkrishnapur 
Division, who also do the same work ns the two claimants 
referred to by him, tbrd the only job of Blacksmith that 
they do is of soldering the ends of the saw blades, that they 
also sharpen the teeth cf the saw blade on the power driven 
grinding stone, that they do not do any other Carpentry 
job (hat the Carpenters and Blacksmiths were in old Cate- 
gories V to IX whereas the Sawyers were only in old Cate- 
gory V, that the^e Sawyers arc semi-skilled, that when he was 
p.t Kothagudcm there was conciliation when the Sawyers these 
demanded new Category IV and finally that demand was re- 
jected by the Government of India in 1968 and so the new 
Category for Sawyers at Kothagudein is III, that when he 
was at Kcthagudem, Ydlandu Sawyers also demanded new 
Category IV and that demand was elso rejected, that the 
job content, of the duties of the Sawyers are the same now as 
it war prior to Wage Board, that by the nature of the duties 
done by Sawyers they are rot entitled to any higher cate- 
gory, that after the implementation of the Wage Board re- 
commendations these two claimants and others made some 
r, 'presentation through the Union, that Hx. M3 is the copy 
of that representations dated 2-7-1970 made to the Assistant 
Labour Commissioner (Central). In his cross examination he 
says that Blacksmiths who were ia old Category V were 
given now Category IV after Wage Board that he does not 
know whether Carpenters were also in Old Category IV 
prior to Wage Board, that Carpenters who were in old cate- 
gory V were given new Category IV after Wage Board, that 
even in the Coal Award no designation was given to the 

Sawyer, that he docs not know on what basis the Sawyers 

were given Category TU when Blacksmiths and Carpenters in 
Old Category V were given now Category IV and that the 
grinding stone is in the timber yard itself and the Sawyers 
Sharpen the teeth of the blades, that the work of soldering 

tho ends of the blades is of Blacksmith, that he does not 

know whether previously sharpening the teeth of the saw 
blades was done in the workshop, but now no work is done 
uy the workshop ?.o far ns sharpening and oilier work are 
concerned, that he does net know whether Muecadams in the 
1 imber Yard were previously in Old Category III when the 



1056 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MARCH 3, 1973/PHALGUNA 12, 1894 


[Part II— 


Sawyers were in old Category V and that he does not know 
what new Category was given to the Muccadams in the 
Timber Yard. No doubt some evidence also has been let in 
through this witness to show that the work of Timber cut- 
ting has come down and that there is not much work for 
the Sawyers and there has been also some cross examination 
on this aspect but this aspect of the matter is not relevant 
for the purpose of the present enquiry, because the whole 
question whether giving of new Category III to the 
claimants is justified or not 

9. Now from the evidence referred to it is clear that the 
claimants were previously working under a contract and 
that later on the Company absorbed them, that they were 
designated as Band-Saw Cutters, that they were placed in 
old Category V, that their main work is only of cutting 
timber into required sizes and that incidentally they arc 
also sharpening the teeth of the saw blades and also solder- 
ing the saw blades whenever necessity arose, and that the 
Wage Board did not make any specific recommendations so 
far as these Band-Saw Cutters are concerned and that 
at the time of implementation of the recommendations of 
the Wage Board the Management designated the climants as 
Sawyers and placed them in new category III and that the 
management has also placed in category III other workers who 
were in old categories IV and V. It is now seen from page 
169 of Volume I of the recommendations of the Wage Board 
that as Regards Wage Structure the Wage Board had 
recommended that the existing ten categories should be com- 
pressed into six categories as shown in Appendix V and this 
Appendix V is contained in pages 41 to 50 of Volume II of 
the recommendations of the Wage Board. A perusal of Ap- 
pendix V shows that the Wage Board had recommended cate- 
gory II for Semi-skiLled lower and Category III for Semi- 
skilled higher. In as much as the Wage Board has not made 
any specific recommendations so far as Band-Saw Cutters 
are concerned, considering the nature of the work done by the 
claimants, the Management had fixed them in new Category III. 
Even W.W.l admits that their work has no comparison with 
the work of Blacksmiths and Can>enters So if the Blacksmiths 
and Carpenters are given some higher category considering the 
nature of the work done by them, the claimants cannot claim 
equal category merely on the ground that sharpening teeth 
of the blades and soldering the saw blades is the blacksmith’s 
work that they arc doing and that the cutting of timber 
also is the work like the carpenter’s work and so when the 
blacksmiths and carpenters were given new category IV 
they also should be given new Category IV. 

10. From the evidence it is seen that the nature of work 

done by the claimants is only semi-skilled work and even 
if it Is assumed that for sharpening teeth of the saw blades 
and for soldering the saw blades some skill is required then 
their work would come only under semi-skilled higher in 
instead of semi-skiJlcl lower and now it is seen that the Wage 
Board has recommended Category III for the workers doing 
the work of Semi-skillel higher. No doubt it is also now 
seen from the evidence that when similar requests were made 
by the Sawyers of Kothagudem and Yellandu the Government 
of India had turned down their request observing that the 
Government of India did not consider the dispute fit for re- 
ference and no doubt in view of this fact it is contended for 
the respondent that the claim of the petitioners also is liable 
to be rejected, but simply because the Government of India 
did not think it fit to refer such a dispute to this tribunal it 
does not mean that the present claimants are debarred from 
putting forward their claim. Now the matter has come up 
before this Tribunal, and it is for this Tribunal to give a 
finding as to whether the Management is justified in placing 
the claimants in new Category III, after considering the evi- 
dence placed before It. Now from the evidence let in this 

case it is clear that the nature of the work done by the 

claimants is at best only that of semi-skilled higher, but 
it cannot be said that their work is that of a skilled junior. 
It is now seen that the Wage Board had recommended cate- 
gory IV to skilled junior. On a consideration of the whole 
evidence in this case and also the recommendations of the 
Wage Board I am satisfied that considering the nature of 
the work done by the claimants the Management had rightly 

fitted the claimants in Category III new. 

11. For all the aforesaid reasons I hold on the dispute re- 
ferred to for adjudication that the Management of Bellampalli 
and Ramakrishnapur Divisions of Messrs. Singareni Col- 
lieries Company Limited, is justified in placing Sarvashri 
Nagula Chandraiah, Kannaboyina Posham, Kusina Lingaiah, 
Dandi Mondi,Gujja Bucham, Band Saw Cutters in Category 


III under the new Wage Board recommendations and that 
the claimants are not entitled to any relief in these pro- 
ceedings. 

Award is passed accordingly. 

Dictated to the Stenographer, transcribed by him and 
corrected by me and given under my hand and the seal of 
this Tribunal, this the 17th day of January, 1973. 

INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL, 
P. S. ANANTH, Presiding Officer.

Bombay ;

arbitration agreement has been forwarded to the Central 
Government;

10 A ol the said Act, the Central Government hereby pub- 
lishes the said arbitration agreement which was received by 
it on the 3rd February, 1973.

of the Association for payment of Light Roferesh-

CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL- 
CUM-LABOUR COURT AT JABALPUR 

Dated January 22. 1973 

Present: 

Mr. Justice S, N. Kalju — Presiding Officer. 

Case Ref. No. CGIT/LC(R)/72 
(Notification No. 1/57/68-LRII dated 26-7-1972) 

Parties: 

Employers in relation to the management of the North, 
South and West Jhagrakhand Collieries, Post Office 
Jhagrakhand Collieries, District Surguja (Madhya 
Pradesh) and their workmen represented through 
the Madhya Pradesh Colliery Workers’ Federation. 
P, O, Chirimiri, District Surguja (M.P.) 

Appearances : 

For employers — S/Sri P, S, Nair and G. R. Bhandari 

For workmen— Sri Gulab Gupta, 

Industry: Coal Mine District: Surguja (M. P.) 

AWARD 

This Is a reference under Section 10(1) (d) of the Indus- 
trial Disputes Act, 1947. The question referred to this Tri- 
bunal as set out in the schedule to the reference is: — 

“Whether the demand of the workmen of the North. 
South and West Jhagrakhand Collieries represented 
by the Madhya Pradesh Colliery Workers Federa- 
tion, post Office Chirimiri, District Surguja (Madhya 
Pradesh) for full implementation of all the recom- 
mendations of the Wage Board for Coal Mining 
Industry as accepted by Government including pay- 
ment of Variable Dearness Allowance at the rates 
as applicable from time to time; by the manage- 
ments of the North, South and West Jhagrakhand 
Collieries, Post Office Jhagrakhand Collieries, Dis- 
trict Surguja (Madhya Pradesh) is justified? If so, 
to what relief arc the workmen entitled?” 

Disputes of similar nature were involved in three other 
references relating to West Chirimiri and New Chirimiri 


Ponri Hill Collieries (Reference No. 10 of 1969). 
North Chirimiri Colliery (Reference No. 23 of 1969) 
and Chirimiri Colliery (Reference No. 2 of 1972), In the 
aforesaid three references the parties resolved the disputes 
amicably between them. The terms of the settlements in the 
three references were found by me to be fair and reasonable 
and I gave my awards in term3 of aforesaid settlements bet- 
ween the parties. The award in Reference No. 10 of 1969 
was in two parts and was given on 14-12-1971 and 18-9-1972, 
The award in Reference No. 23 of 1969 was given by me on 
4-9-1972 and in Reference No. 2 of 1972 the award was 
given by me on 3-11-1972. The parties, however, could not 
resolve their dispute in the present reference before me. 

The proceedings in the present reference before me have 
been some what protracted. Earlier, 30 workers of the 
South Jhagrakhand Collieries (hereinafter called the Jhagni- 
khand Collieries) presented an application, No, 200 of 1968, 
under Sec. 33-C(2) of the Industrial Disputes Act 1947 
(hereinafter called the Act). In the said application the ap- 
plicants claimed the difference in the Variable Dearness Al- 
lowance as determined in the Central Wage Board Avvard 
which they contended was due to them. The operative por- 
tion of the order of this Court dated 22-3-1969 runs thus: — 

“The result is that all the applicants are entitled to the 
sum of Rs. 75.60 P, as claimed till the date of ap- 
plication. They shall further be entitled to Rs. 5/- 
each in all Rs. 150/- as costs of the proceedings 
from the opposite party." 

The aforesaid order was challenged by the management 
of the Jhagrakhand Collieries by way of a writ petition be- 
fore the High Court of Madhya Pradesh. It was dismissed 
by the High Court by its order dated 21-9-1971. 

It may be mentioned that the application under Sec. 33-C(2) 
in Case No. CGIT/LC(C) (472) 1970 which was decided by 
this Court on 7-1-1970 had been opposed by the manage- 
ment on the ground, inter aha, that a settlement, had been 
arrived at in conciliation proceedings between the manage- 
ment and another Union, other than the M.P. Colliery 

Workers Federation which had sponsored the dispute in the 
aforesaid application, and the affect of the settlement was 
that the matter had come to an end on account of the afore- 
said agreement in concilation proceedings. It was contended 
on behalf of the workmen that Sri B. D. Sharma before whom 
the alleged settlement was said to have been arrived at was 
not a duly appointed Conciliation Officer on the date when 
the settlement was said to have been arrived at between the 
parties. This Court held that Sri B. D. Sharma was not a 
duly appointed Conciliation Officer on the material date. 
The question was raised before the High Court and the 

view of this Court was upheld by the High Court and it held 
that the aforesaid conciliation proceedings were of no affect 
and the settlement arrived at in such proceedings was not 

binding on all the workers who were not parties to those 

proceedings. Lt further expressed the view that the afore- 
said settlement was not binding “upon the respondent work- 
ers because they had already made an application before the 
Labour Court and while this lis was pending, it was not open 
to the management to settle with other workers and to make 
the settlement binding upon the workers who had gone before 
the Labour Court and thus to frustrate their proceedings. 
In the result the High Court dismissed the writ petition, It 
appears that the aforesaid order of the High Court has been 
subjected to an appeal which is pending before the Supreme 
Court of India. The Supreme Court has also stayed further 
proceedings in the said application under Sec. 33-C(2) of the 
Act. As against the aforesaid order of the High Court dated 
21-9-1971 it was stated before me that, an application for 
leave to appeal from the aforesaid order before the Supremo 
Court of India is pending before the High Court. 

The management has challenged; the competency of the 
reference before me, particularly on the ground L that the 
Central Wage Board recommendations for Coal Mining In- 
dustries on which the claim of the workmen is based related 
to about 800 collieries in different Slates of India and the 
question involved in the reference being one of National im- 
portance and an inter state matter should not have been 
referred to this Tribunal and instead the appropriate forum 
for the consideration of the dispute raised in the reference 
before me should have been a National Tribunal, There may 
be some force in this contention but in view of the awards 
based on mutual settlements that have been given by me in 
the three other references in which the same question was 



Sec, 3(ii)] 


1059 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MARCH 3, 1973/PHALGUNA 12, 1894 


involved I do not think it is necessary for me to go further 
into this question, 

The learned representative of the workmen argued that tho 
recommendations of the Wage Board were initially accepted by 
the management and they have become part of the condi- 
tions of service of the workmen and the demands of the work- 
men had already been allowed by this Court in the aforesaid 
applications under Sec. 33-C(2) of the Act and the orders 
of this Court had been upheld by the High Court of Madhya 
Pradesh. He contended that the aforesaid orders had the 
force of resjudicata and the objections raised by the man- 
agement could not be agitated again. It was contended on 
behalf of the management that the aforesaid orders wore 
passed in the course of proceedings under Sec. 33-C(2) of 
the Act and they have no binding effect at all in proceed- 
ings under Sec. 10 of tho Act. It was also contended that 
the question was now before the Supreme Court of India 
and in one of the applications the order of the High Court has 
been stayed by the Supreme Court. It was contended that 
after the aforesaid orders of the High Court of Madhya Pia- 
desh the workers of the Jhagrnkhand Collieries reaffirmed 
the settlement dated 22-10-1969 which had been reached in 
course of the aforesaid conciliation proceedings by individual- 
ly accepting the terms of the settlement and by accepting a 
lump-sum payment of Rs, 3,20,000 towards arrears of Vari- 
able Dearness Allowance for the period beginning from 
1-4-1968 to 30-9-1969. It was urged that by such individual 
acceptance a fresh contract came into existence by which the 
earlier settlement of 22-10-69 was again accepted by the 
workmen as binding on the parties. 

Receipts signed by 4351 workmen have been produced by 
the management. Notice of change of service conditions 
under Sec. 9 A of the Act was issued on 26-6-1970. The an- 
nexure to the aforesaid notice said: — 

“By settlement dated 22-10-1969 arrived at before the 
Assistant Labour Commissioner. Shahdol. the Dis- 
pute for V.D.A. for the period from 1-4-68 to 
30-9-1969 was fully and finally settled. It was fur- 
ther agreed in the said settlement that the workmen 
will be paid V.D.A. @ Rs, 1.29 with effect from 
1-10-69. 

In pursuance of the above settlement, notices were 
issued by the management on 28-10-69 and 22-1-69 
and the workmen have accepted payment of 
V,DA. @ Rg. 1.29 in terms of the said settle- 
ment. 

Willion t prejudice to the employer's contention that 
notice under Section 9 A is not required and that 
the notice dated 18-10-67 did not create a con- 
dition of service enabling the workmen to claim 
V.D.A. at a rate higher than Rs. 1.11 per day, as 
a matter of abundant precaution in order to avoid 
any further controversy and/or plea on the part 
of any workman concerned, the employer hereby 
notifies the following changes : — 

In supersession of its notices dated 18-10-67 and 
14-1-69.* the employer shall pay to the workmen a 
fixed V.D.A. ^ Rs. 1.29 per day until further 
notice.” 

The management have filed a copy of the conciliation 
settlement dated 22-10-1969. copies of the aforesaid settle- 
ments in the above mentioned three references, and a copy 
of (he settlement dated 21-10-1972 between the manage- 
ment of Jhagrakhand Collieries and their workmen repre- 
sented by M.P. Colliery Workers’ Federation and the M.P. 
Koyla Mazdur Sabha in which it was agreed that the demand 
relating to the Variable Dearness Allowance shall be settled by 
bipartite talks. In an affidavit which was filed along with 
the aforesaid documents it was stated that the documents 
which had been produced were copies of the relevant docu- 
ments. The learned representative of the workmen objected 
to tho admissibility of the aforesaid documents. The said 
documents supported by an affidavit were filed before me 
on 19-12-1972. The order sheet says : — 

“Sri P. S. Nair appears for the management and files 
23 documents with list along with affidavit of 
Shri G. R, BhandarJ for proving the documents. 
Documents are marked Exhibits E/1 to E/23”. 


Thereafter a rejoinder was filed on behalf of the workmen 
on 20-12-1972. The representatives of the parties stated 
before me on 20-12-1972 that they will not produce any 
witnesses. The admissibility of the aforesaid documents 
was not specifically challenged by the workmen. All that 
was stated in tho rejoinder was : — 

“The Federation submits that the management should 
not be allowed to introduce irrelevant material 
with a view to prejudice the case before this 
Hon’ble Tribunal and this Hon’blc Tribunal should 
decide the issue on the basis of the material sub- 
mitted by the Federation,” 

Thus the aforesaid documents were sought to be proved 
by the management by an affidavit of Shri G. R. Bhaadari. 
It was open to the workmen either to challenge the suffi- 
ciency of the proof of the aforesaid documents or to have 
demanded the production of the originals of any of the 
copies of the documents which had been filed by the 
management, Rule 15 of the Rules of the Act says that 
this Tribunal “may accept, admit or call for evidence at 
any_ stage of the proceedings before it and in such manner 
as it may think fit.” In the present case documents had 
been filed by the management In full knowledge of the 
workmen's representative. Fie did not specifically challenge 
the proof of the documents by the affidavit of the manage- 
ment, nor did he raise any objection to the aforesaid 
documents being accepted by the Tribunal by its order 
dated 19-12-1972. It was open to the representative of the 
workmen to have raised an objection that proof by an 
affidavit was not sufficient and the management should pro- 
duce further evidence to prove the documents which had 
been produced by It. No such thing was done and all 
that was contended on behalf of the workmen was that the 
management “should not be allowed to introduce irrelevant 
material” before the Tribunal. It was for the Tribunal to 
see whether the evidence was material or not. So far as 
ihe admissibility of the documents was concerned it was 
not challenged on behalf of the workmen. The Supreme 
Court in Sindh u Resettlement Corporation, Ltd'. Vs. Indus- 
trial Tribunal, Gujarat, and others (1968-I-LIJ n, 834) took 
the view that in proceedings before the Industrial Tribunal 
strict proof of documents in accordance with the provisions 
of the Indian Evidence Act is not required. There 
is, therefore, no force in the contention of the workmen's 
representative that the aforesaid documents had not been 
proved and therefore they should not be accepted in evi- 
dence. 

The learned representative of the workmen placed reliance 
on Burn & Company Ltd. Vs. Their workmen (A.I.R. 1957 
(SC) 38) and Walford Transport Ltd. Vs. First Industrial 
Tribunal of West Bengal and others (1963 AIR (Calcutta) 
275) in support of the contention that principle of resjudi- 
cota applies to industrial disputes and the decision of this 
Court and the M.P. High Court in the earlier applications 
under Sec, 33-C(2) of the Act should be accepted as final, 
Apart from the question whether the aforesaid orders of 
this Court and the M.P. High Court in proceedings under 
See. 33-C(2) of the Act have any binding effect in pro- 
ceedings under Sec. 10 of the Act, it is now well settled 
that technical^ considerations of resjudicata cannot be regid- 
ly followed in industrial disputes. In (1964-I-LLJ p, 380) 
Workmen of Balmer Lawric & Co. vs. BaJmer Lawrie 
& Co. Ltd. and another, the Supreme Court observed : — 

“While dealing with the question about the revision of 
Wage Scales, it is necessary to remember that 
the technical considerations of resjudicata should 
not be allowed to hamper the discretion of indus- 
trial adjudication." 

Again, the Supreme Court, in the case of Shahdara Saharan- 
pur Light Railway Vs. Shahdara Saharanpur Railway 
Workers’ Union (1969-I-LLJ p. 734) observed: — 

“the trend in recent decisions is that application of 
technical rules such as resjudicata, accjuiescnce, 
estoppel etc. are not appropriate to industrial 
adjudication.” 

Therefore, where the circumstances so warrant, thb Tribunal 
would not be fettered by the technical rules of resjudicata

and it will be open to it to come to its own decision while the parties. The Supreme Court in SIrsilk Ltd. Vs. Govern- 

giving its award. It was stated by the management that:— ment of Andhra Pradesh (1963-IT-LLJ p.647) observed: — 


“The employers in the instant case are owners of a 
group of collieries consisting of North, South and 
West Jhagrakhand Collieries, all situated in the 
Korea Coalfield in Surguja District of Madhya 
Pradesh and employ about 3,900 workers. The 
Korea Coalfield consists of 5 collieries in public 
sector and 8 collieries in private sector. The col- 
licries in the private sector are as under i —

6. North Jhagrakhand Colliery. 

7. South Jhagrakhand Colliery. 

8. West Jhagrakhand Colliery. 

There are about 49 collieries in the State of Madhya 
Pradesh.” 


The dispute between the employers and the worker* of 
the West Chirimiri Colliery, New Chirimiri Ponri Hill Col- 
liery and Chirimiri Colliery which had given rise to Refer- 
ence Nos. 10 of 1969, 23 of 1969 and 2 of 1972 were 
amicably settled between the parties including the M.P. 
Colliery Workers Federation and this Tribunal, finding the 
terms of the settlements fair and reasonable, gave awards 
in accordance with the terms of the aforesaid settlements.

It has been contended on behalf of the management that 
the conditions in the aforesaid collieries and the Jhagrakhand 
Collieries being similar there is no reason why there should 
be variations m the above rates with regard only in the 
Jhagrakhand Collieries. It was argued that under the terms 
of settlement of 1969 which was subsequently agreed upon 
individually by the workers of the Jhagrakhand Collieries 
the workers were given less favourable terms as compared 
to the terms which were later mutually agreed upon between 
the parties in the aforesaid three references, but now the 
management was willing to pay the Variable Dearness 
Allowance in accordance with the aforesaid rates which 
were agreed upon between the parties in the aforesaid three 
references. It was, however, contended that under the 
terms of 1969 settlement the workmen were paid at the 
rate of Rs. 1.29 per day from 1-4-1968 to 31-7-1968 instead 
of Rs. 1.11 per day as agreed upon in the settlements of 
the aforesaid three references. Tt was argued on behalf of 
the workmen that the Variable Dearness Allowance had 
been fixed earlier by the management of the Jhagrakhand 
Collieries and it had become a condition of service and any 
change was permissible only on the basis of altered condi- 
tions and there was no justification for the management 
to make any change in the conditions of sendee of the 
workmen. It was also stressed that since the management 
was now admittedly paying Rs. 2.13 per day there was no 
reason why payments of the Variable Dearness Allowance 
should be paid at a lesser rate for the period of the claim. 
As mentioned above, it has come in evidence that the 
management of the Jhagrakhand Collieries had duly issued 
a notice under See. 9A of the Act and there was a fresh 
contract between the workmen and the management after 
the settlement of 1969 as a result of the individual workers 
having agreed to receive payment at the rates mutually 
agreed upon by the parties There is nothing to prevent 
this Tribunal from giving effect to a settlement between 


“Ordinarily there should be no difficulty about the 
matter, for if a settlement has been arrived at 
between the parties while the dispute is pending 
before the Tribunal, ^ the parties would file the 
settlement and the Tribunal would make the award 
in accordance with the settlement.” 


Furthermore, an industrial tribunal acting under Sec. 10(1) 
of the Act is not bound by any contract of service in de- 
ciding a reference. The Supreme Court in Western India 
Automobile Association Vs. Industrial Tribunal Bombay 
(1949-LLJ p. 245) observed;^ 

“Adjudication does not, in our opinion, mean adjudi- 
cation according to the strict law of master and 
servant. The award of the tribunal may contain 
provision for settlement of a dispute which no 
court could order if it was bound by ordinary 
law, but the tribunal is not fettered in any way 
by these limitations ” 

The same view was expressed by the Supreme Court in 
Rohtas Industries Ltd. Vs Brijnandan Pandey dt others 
(1956-II-LLJ 444). It was observed : — 

“An industrial tribunal is not so fettered and may 
create new obligations or modify contracts in the 
interests of industrial peace . . . ” 

The Supreme Court expressed the following view in the 
New Maneckshowk Spinning & Weaving Company (1961-1- 
LLJ 521) : — 

“there is no doubt, therefore, that it is open to an 
industrial court in an appropriate case to impose 
new obligations on the parties before it or modify 
contracts in the interest of industrial peace or give 
awards which may have the effect of extending 
existing agreement or making a new one.” 

It is not possible for me to overlook the fact that disputes 
involving the same question which is now before me were 
mutually settled between the parties in the aforesaid three 
references and I have given my awards in terms of the 
settlements arrived at between the parties. Any variation 
from the aforesaid terms of settlements would create glar- 
ing anomaly in the rates of Variable Dearness Allowance 
payable to the workmen and is bound to raise industrial 
unrest. This Tribunal has to see that harmony and peace 
is maintained. Furthermore, the principle of uniformity 
cannot be overlooked. Under these circumstances, I am not 
prepared to make any distinction in the rates of the Vari- 
able Dearness Allowance paid to the workmen of the 
jhagrakhand Collieries and the workmen of the other 
neighbouring collieries in which the same question which is 
now in issue before me was mutually settled between the 
parties as mentioned above. I, therefore, consider it proper 
that the terms with regards to rates of the Variable Dear- 
ness Allowance in the aforesaid three references should 
also be made applicable in the present reference. The 
aforesaid terms have, however, to be varied to the extent 
that they are brought in conformity with the rates of Vari- 
able Dearness Allowance payable tt> the workmen as 
directed in my awards in the aforesaid three references. 

My answer to the reference, therefore, is that Variable 
Dearness Allowance shall be paid to the workmen on rolls 
of the Colliery and at the rates as mentioned below : — 

Period Rate of V.D.A. per day, 

1-4-1968 to 31-7-1968 Rs. 1,11 

L8-1968 to 30-9-1969 Rs. 1.29 

MO-1969 to 31-7-1971 Rs. 1.29 

1-8-1971 to 31-5-1972 Rs. 1.60 

1-6-1972 to 30-9-1972 Rs. 2.13 

Since the workmen have been paid at the rate of Rs. 1.29 
per day from 1-4-1968 to 31-7-1968 instead of at the rate

of R«. 1.11 per day the exceei amount (hall be deducted 
from the amounts due to the workmen at the aforesaid 
rate# from 1*4-1968 to 30-9-1972 and I make my award 
accordingly. The parties will bear their own costs.
